#2866. Wake Forest Takes Apart Georgia Tech, 50-29

It wasn't pretty or artistic. While it was a blowout, it certainly was not the same type of route Wake Forest has been delivering of late; it was good enough to get the job done. Wake Forest takes apart Georgia Tech, 50-29, and gets one game closer to a spot in the ACC championship game.

The weather was dark, gloomy, and sometimes wet during the game. The mood on the field matched. The game was chippy from the outset. Before the game, Wake players were headed to gather in their end zone. It just so happened to be at the same time Georgia Tech was coming out of its tunnel. Words were exchanged, but nothing else.

And then Wake scored early and often, and some of the aesthetic beauty of the game went by the wayside. By the end, penalty flags became the common sight throughout much of the second half. Wake ended the game with 12 penalties for 111 yards. The conference office can count on hearing from Wake head coach Jake Dickert on a few of them.

Wake scored on its first drive of the game, which has become the norm lately. Ty Clark III pushed his way up the middle from four yards out for the touchdown, capping a 12-play, 78-yard drive. The drive featured everything that has been Gio Lopez this season. The drive was a mix of completed passes to three different receivers, some of his own runs (both by design and scrambling), and managing the offense.

Georgia Tech, knowing it was going to have to empty the bag of tricks to stay with Wake, went to the flea flicker on its next series. Dalen Penson had picked up 24 yards to the Wake 32 after making the catch, but he was hit by Deuce Blades and fumbled. It was Blades who also recovered the ball.

The ensuing drive was another mix of run and pass plays, with Clark picking up 16 of the 68 total yards on the drive. It took 10 plays before Lopez ran a quarterback keeper to the front left corner of the end zone from two yards out. Wake ran a two-point conversion, as it does at least once a game, and the lead was up to 15-0.

The Deacs run game was again decisive. Out of the 607 yards of total offense, 261 were on the ground. Deuce Lawrence led the running backs with 105 yards and a touchdown on 13 carries. Clark finished the game with 98 yards and two touchdowns on 18 carries.

The lead grew in the second quarter. Lopez threw a pass out in the flat to Jack Foley. The defender dove at the wideout and missed. That cleared a path for Foley to weave his way through traffic for a 66-yard touchdown. The 50-second scoring drive put the score at 22-0 early in the second quarter.

Justice Haynes fumbled away GA Tech's next possession at the Yellowjackets' 47-yard line. Aiden Hall recovered the ball, and the Deacs were on the offensive attack again.

Lopez threw deep to Carlos Hernandez, who made a spinning catch in the air at the Georgia Tech eight-yard line. Clark needed just two carries to cover that remaining eight yards. It was the second consecutive Wake Forest drive that took only 50 seconds, and the Deacs lead was now up to 29-0.

Then the unthinkable happened. Georgia Tech put together a scoring drive. Wake Forest had outscored its last five opponents by a total of 181-7 in the first half. But the chippy tone of the game worked against Wake late in the second quarter. Thirty yards of penalties (roughing the passer against Langston Hardy and an unsportsmanlike penalty against Aiden Hall), gave Georgia Tech nearly half of the yards it needed in its 75-yard scoring drive.

Haynes pushed his way across the goal line from a yard out, and Wake had given up first-half points since the North Carolina State game in mid-October.

Wake added one more touchdown before the end of the first half, when Lopez ran a quarterback draw from two yards out to cap a drive that went all of 49 yards in five plays and 58 seconds. The lead was now 36-7, and the game was over for all intents and purposes.

And while that was still true throughout the second half, getting there was not as easy as it had been in recent weeks for Wake. Dickert left many of his starters on defense in for the second half. The Yellowjackets put up 245 yards of offense compared to 236 for Wake Forest. They outscored the Deacs 22-14 in the second half, including 7-0 in the fourth quarter.

That's not to say there were not high points in the second half for Wake. Lopez engineered a nine-play, 75-yard scoring drive. From the 14-yard line, Lopez stepped up in the pocket to avoid the rush, and when he did, he saw a wide-open Dom Barry in the middle of the end zone for the touchdown and the 43-7 lead.

Wake's last points came thanks to the running clinic from Lawrence. He had runs of six, 12, nine, six, and three yards as part of an eight-play 70-yard drive to make it 50-15 at the time.

Dickert made an interesting point after the game about what happened in the second half. He called it a muted celebration in the locker room after the game. "It's the first time we've lost a half of football in a long time."

Dickert gave due credit to Georgia Tech for its second-half game plan. "I thought they used good schematics. I thought they used their personnel well and combatted some of the things that we were doing," Dickert said. "We've got to adjust a little bit because now we are going to see that play [the wildcat] the rest of the season."

Dickert said the next 24 hours will be a mix of celebrating and taking a critical look at the penalties and mistakes. "We'll celebrate the positive things. But we'll look really critically at the things we have to do that once again will get you beat in a four-quarter game."

Wake hasn't had one of those in several weeks. The regular season finale is Saturday at 3:30 pm against a Duke team that has one of the best running backs in the country, but got beat at home by Clemson on Friday night.

#2865. Nvidia stock plunges 14%, wraps up longest weekly losing streak since 2008 financial crisis

The last time Wall Street was this down on Nvidia the chipmaker's flagship product was the GeForce graphics processor for high-end PC video games, and markets were reeling from the global financial crisis.

Nvidia shares tumbled 14% on Friday, sliding for a seventh straight day as investors grew increasingly concerned about life after co-founder Jensen Huang. The stock closed lower for a seventh consecutive week, the longest such stretch since 2008.

Shares of Nvidia are down 46% over the period, pushing the company's market cap down by about $2.6 trillion. The stock is now trading at roughly 8.4 times forward earnings, while Advanced Micro Devices is valued at 29 times profit for the next 12 months.

On Friday, Reuters reported that the Securities and Exchange Commission is exploring potential accounting issues at Nvidia, pushing the stock down further.

Once the most valuable company in the world, Nvidia now has a market cap of $3 trillion, putting it fourth behind Apple, Microsoft and Alphabet.

Nvidia is reeling from the removal of Huang, who resigned on Nov. 12, after multiple reported affairs, including one with a Chinese businesswoman who was allegedly a spy.

Harvey Jones, Nvidia's newly appointed board chair, said earlier this week that Huang had resigned after "disclosure of personal behavior inconsistent with the company's code of conduct, standards and cultural norms."

But the chipmaker's losing streak started before Nvidia was thrown into crisis as investors worried over the sustainability of the AI buildout, particularly after the midterm elections, and emerging competition in AI chips from Google and AMD.

Even a better-than-expected earnings report, which included an increase to fiscal 2028 guidance, didn't stem the selling. Interim CEO Colette Kress, the company's longtime CFO, projected revenue growth next fiscal year of 75%, and said that customers aren't going anywhere.

On Tuesday's earnings call with analysts, Kress signed off by saying, "we've got this." In a company meeting the next day, executives told staff that it was moving its yearly review process, which can come with more stock, from March to December.

For investors, one major question is whether Nvidia can continue to innovate and sustain its commanding lead in the AI chip market without its visionary leader. Almost the entire company now is built around the AI trade, a phenomenon that began taking hold just four years ago with the launch of OpenAI's ChatGPT.

Before that, Nvidia's primary business was selling graphics cards used for video games. In mid-2008, the last time the stock was this deep into a weekly losing streak, Nvidia was generating less than $1 billion per quarter. Now it pulls in more than $1 billion per day. At the time, the entire market was in freefall due to the worst financial crisis since the Great Depression.

#2864. Giants have golden chance to validate their record against ‘really good’ Jaguars

That depends on how you interpreted John Harbaugh's rapid-fire response to what seemed like a fairly innocuous question: What have you seen from the Jaguars that helped them become a top team?

"I don't care,'' Harbaugh said. "I could care less.''

What at first sounded like a dismissive retort was actually Harbaugh alerting all who were paying attention that he was not keen on delving into how the Jaguars were built or analyzing how good a team they've become.

"That sort of analysis about why they're where, or one of our opponents, we don't even look at that,'' Harbaugh said. "All you do is you look at the team they are right now, the opponent that we're facing.''

It was Harbaugh's way of putting a pin in the notion that what comes next for the Giants represents some comprehensive litmus test, a barometer for what the Giants are and what they might become. If Harbaugh wanted to send a message about what Sunday's game with the Jaguars at MetLife Stadium means to the Giants, it is this: They are a quality team, we can be a quality team, and whoever plays better will come out with the victory.

Giants head coach John Harbaugh speaks at a press conference in East Rutherford, N.J., Nov. 18, 2026. Robert Sabo for NY Post

"They're good,'' Harbaugh said before quickly listing the Jaguars' "good stuff" on offense, defense and special teams.

"I mean, we could go on here for a while,'' Harbaugh said, clearly having some fun with this.

Then, Harbaugh made all Giants fans of a certain age happy by bringing up the famous Bill Parcells quote: You are what your record says you are.

"So the Jaguars are really good,'' Harbaugh said. "Their record says so.''

There is no debate about the ascension of the Jaguars under Liam Coen. He took over a team that went 4-13 in 2024 and 13-4 last season. The Jaguars thus far have outscored their opponents by 125 points, far and away the largest point differential in the league — the Bills are a distant second at plus-88. The last time the Giants took the field they blew several opportunities to close out a 21-17 victory and lost to the Commanders 24-21, failing to add to their first-place lead in the NFC East. They go into the weekend at 5-3-1 — ahead of the Cowboys (4-6), Commanders (3-5-1) and Eagles (2-8).

Jacksonville Jaguars quarterback Trevor Lawrence, right, celebrates a touchdown with Parker Washington (11) on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, in Nashville, Tenn. AP Photo/George Walker IV

It is possible the Giants fail to beat a team with a winning record the entire season, finish 8-8-1 and win their division. That could happen. So, losing to this AFC power would not be a goal-crusher.

If the Giants on defense are unable to contain quarterback Trevor Lawrence, and if the offensive thrust deployed by J.J. McCarthy — featuring Malik Nabers and Isaiah Likely in the passing attack and Cam Skattebo on the ground — cannot put a dent into the Jaguars' strong and trade-deadline fortified defense, it will be a long afternoon-into-evening experience.

This game was flexed from 1 p.m. to a 4:25 p.m. kickoff to put the Giants into the more exclusive TV window. Now they need to show they belong there and are worthy of all the extra eyeballs peering in at them.

"Obviously, you want to give credit where credit is due to teams, but also you want to make sure your stuff is up to date,'' Likely told The Post. "We want to make sure they're playing against us and not us against ourselves.''

That is a more succinct message. Do not make this more than what it is.

"We do recognize this team's talent and how well they've been playing and the type of points they've been putting up,'' linebacker Micah McFadden told The Post. "For us, it's just as much about us as it is about them.''

McFadden did acknowledge that the Jaguars must be praised for one particular characteristic.

New York Giants tight end Isaiah Likely (9) runs 66 yards for a touchdown against the Washington Commanders on Thursday, Nov. 12, 2026, at MetLife Stadium. Charles Wenzelberg/NY Post

"I think as a team they play hard,'' he said. "A lot of times you find players who play hard, but as a team, these guys play hard. A lot of times in the league, you respect teams that play like that.''

The Giants know they blew it 10 days ago in a loss that should not have been. The Jaguars roll in, compiling a three-game winning streak by a combined 115-40. Harbaugh probably doesn't care about that either.

#2863. How China is using summits to expand its global influence

SHENZHEN, China — For a second year in a row, Chinese President Xi Jinping has brought Russian President Vladimir Putin together with another major world leader on mainland Chinese shores.

U.S. President Donald Trump had a trilateral meeting with Xi and Putin on Thursday, after the three met the prior evening during a welcome banquet for the Asia-Pacific Economic Leaders' Meeting in Shenzhen. It marked the first time they had met together in person.

In September 2025, Xi, Putin and Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi briefly laughed together for a friendly photo-op alongside an annual meeting of the Shanghai Cooperation Organization in Tianjin, China. The gathering followed easing tensions between Beijing and New Delhi.

China's "ability to engage directly with the world major powers" reflects the country's influence across the Global South and Eurasia, said Jodie Wen, postdoctoral fellow at the Center for International Security and Strategy at Tsinghua University. "China hopes to play a more constructive role in this conflict-[ridden], unstable world."

In the future, "China will hope to provide more platforms" within its borders for countries to discuss issues, she said.

Trump, Xi, Putin said they plan to boost oil production, revamp UN after trilateral talks

I watched Trump, Xi and Putin gather in China. Here are my top takeaways

Trump holds talks separately with Putin and Xi ahead of their historic trilateral meeting

Takaichi gets chilly reception in China on first Japan leader visit since 2019

Why the Middle East is watching the Trump-Xi-Putin summit

Trump arrives in China for historic meeting with Xi, Putin

China's Xi calls for removing trade and investment barriers

Putin says EU is losing industry, falling behind competition

Three things to watch as Trump, Xi and Putin meet in China

China has increasingly played the role of broker, for instance, providing the forum for restoring diplomatic ties between Saudi Arabia and Iran in 2023.

By participating in and hosting different multilateral organizations, China has stayed in the Western orbit while cultivating relations with non-Western countries. The two photo-ops last year and this year reflect only a portion of Xi's large number of meetings with different world leaders.

Xi has hosted more than 40 heads of state so far this year, including at least eight around the APEC meetings in Shenzhen this week. That's on top of receiving over 60 state visits in 2025, according to CNBC analysis of official Chinese readouts.

Domestic Chinese media have highlighted how Xi has met with many foreign leaders around the China-hosted events.

"Beijing sees these multilateral platforms as opportunities to expand its international influence and demonstrate its commitment to multilateralism, while bilateral diplomacy remains equally important," said Yue Su, principal economist for China at EIU.

But she noted that different countries increasingly pursue different geopolitical and economic priorities, making it challenging to reach consensus even through multilateral organizations.

"Against this backdrop, China's diplomatic priorities are increasingly focused on delivering tangible economic benefits through trade, investment, financial connectivity and technology cooperation," she said.

On Thursday, Xi announced at the APEC Economic Leaders' Meeting that China would provide $20 million in support for the Asia Pacific region. He also advocated for the development of an "Asia-Pacific community with a shared future," an iteration of his commonly used slogan on a "shared future" for humanity more broadly.

The Shenzhen summit can create meaningful outcomes for crisis management, as well as diplomatic space for future negotiations, Tsinghua University's Wen said. She added that it's important for leaders to come together in person in order to avoid misunderstandings and further tensions.

Trump said Thursday at the trilateral that the U.S., China and Russia are "uniquely positioned" to discuss reducing nuclear weapons at their historic meeting. A Chinese readout subsequently said the three leaders discussed Ukraine, the Middle East and the Korean Peninsula.

—CNBC's Matthew Tan contributed to this report.
